\operatorname{CT}
in
[
8
]
, where
\operatorname{CT}
is defined as a right adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}
.

Moreover, though the right adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}_{B}=p_{*}q^{*}
,
q_{*}p^{!}
can be totally defined in the category of ind-coherent sheaves on
{\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma}
,
{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}
and
{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}
, it is not clear whether the left adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}_{B}
can be totally defined. From the standpoint of the conjectured categorical local Langlands correspondence,
\operatorname{Eis}
on the spectral side corresponds to
\operatorname{Eis}_{!}
on the automorphic side, which does not admit a natural left adjoint.

The
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-bimodules
\Gamma(p_{T}^{*}p_{T*}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})
and
\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})
above are similar to Soergel bimodules. We may consider the following analog of filtrations.
Note that the scheme
T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma}
a union of irreducible components isomorphic to
T_{\sigma}
, indexed by
w\in W^{\sigma}
. We may define the following filtration of
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})
,
which is the counterpart of the Bruhat–Mackey’s filtration on the spectral side.

Definition 2.5.10

(Filtration)
.

Suppose that there is a partial order on
W^{\sigma}
, such that it refines the Bruhat order.
Denote by
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w}
the
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-bimodule with natural action on the left and twisted action by
w
on the right.
The filtration
F^{\geq w}
,
F^{>w}
index by
w\in W^{\sigma}
with respect to the order above of
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})
is defined by

F^{\geq w}:=\bigcap_{w^{\prime}<w}\ker({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})\xrightarrow{(1,w^{\prime})}{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w^{\prime}}).

and

F^{>w}:=\bigcap_{w^{\prime}\leq w}\ker({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})\xrightarrow{(1,w^{\prime})}{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w^{\prime}}).

Taking saturation, we obtain a filtration on
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}
. Since the filtration is compatible with homomorphisms between locally free sheaves in
D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma})_{1}
.
Extending the filtration by linearity,
we obtain uniquely a filtration on
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{F}}
for all
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma})_{1}
. By Proposition
2.5.8
, for any
\chi\in X^{*}(T^{\sigma})^{W^{\sigma}}
, tensoring by
{\mathcal{O}}_{\chi}
defines a filtration on
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{F}}_{\chi}
. In this way, we obtain a filtration on the functor
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}
over
D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma})_{\chi}
.

Moreover, for
B^{\prime}=w^{-1}(B)
, we have

\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}\circ w_{*}

where
W
denotes the
w
-twisting, and the filtration
F^{>w^{\prime}}
of
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}
is defined as
F^{>w^{\prime}w}
of
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}
, under the isomorphism above.

Here we use the term “filtration” for a compatible family of functors, insisting on the fact that for locally free sheaves, it defines a classical filtration in the abelian category
\operatorname{Coh}(T_{\sigma})
. We will see that the piece
F^{w}=F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}
is isomorphic to
w^{*}
, as desired.

Remark 2.5.11

(Geometric intuition of the filtration)
.

We present a geometric intuition of the filtration here. Consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X:={\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\times_{{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}}{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{B}}

For terms in the diagram, we have

{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\simeq G\times^{B}\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{B\sigma}/G\simeq\{(g,h)\in G/B\times\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G\sigma}\mid\mathrm{Ad}(g)^{-1}h\in\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{B\sigma}\}/G,

and

{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\times_{{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}}{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\simeq\{(g_{1},g_{2},h)\in G/B\times G/B\times\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G\sigma}\mid\mathrm{Ad}(g_{1})^{-1}h,\mathrm{Ad}(g_{2})^{-1}h\in\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{B\sigma}\}/G.

Via the projection

{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\times_{{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}}{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\to(G/B\times G/B)/G\simeq B\backslash G/B=\coprod_{w\in W}B\backslash BwB/B,

there is a stratification of
{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\times_{{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}}{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}
indexed by
W
. Moreover, the stratum indexed by
w
is non-empty if and only if
wB\sigma(w)^{-1}\cap B\neq\emptyset
, which is equivalent to
w\in W^{\sigma}
. Thus we obtain a
W^{\sigma}
-stratification of
{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\times_{{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}}{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}
, with respect to the Bruhat order. The filtration satisfies that the
w
-stratum of
X
,
X_{w}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{L}}_{B\cap w^{-1}B\sigma}
by
[
8
]
, Proposition 3.6.1 and then it has a closed substack
{\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma}
.
Thus we may expect that the filtration on
\operatorname{CT}\circ\operatorname{Eis}
can be deduced from the stratification on
{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}\times_{{\mathbb{L}}_{G\sigma}}{\mathbb{L}}_{B\sigma}
in some way.

In
[
8
]
, Theorem 3.6.3, a filtration
\operatorname{CT}_{B*}\operatorname{Eis}_{B}
is defined in this way for the right adjoint
\operatorname{CT}_{B*}
of
\operatorname{Eis}_{B}
.

The subquotients of the filtrations are free
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-modules. More precisely:

Lemma 2.5.12
.

The
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-modules

F^{\geq w}\subset{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})
,
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})/F^{\geq w}
,
F^{>w}\subset{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})
and
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})/F^{>w}
are free
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-modules (by left action). Similarly for the filtration on
\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})
.

More precisely, the
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-module
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})/F^{>w}
is generated by the dual basis
\{\delta_{w^{\prime}}^{*}\}_{w^{\prime}\leq w}
and the
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-module
\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})/F^{>w}
is generated by the dual basis
\{(L\delta)_{w^{\prime}}^{*}\}_{w^{\prime}\leq w}
. Similarly for other modules.

In particular,
\Gamma(F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}))
is free and of rank
1
both for
\operatorname{Eis}
and
\operatorname{eis}
. Moreover, Denote by
\{e_{w}\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})
the (rational) dual basis of
\{(1,w)\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
. Then
L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}({\mathfrak{n}})})^{-1}e_{w}
is a basis of
F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}
for
\operatorname{eis}
and
L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}({\mathfrak{n}})})^{-1}L_{w}^{-1}e_{w}
is a basis of
F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}
for
\operatorname{Eis}
.

Proof.

In the proof, we will denote
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}
to be the set of homomorphisms as left
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-modules.
By definition of the morphism
p_{T*}
, we have an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-bimodules.

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma}),{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}))\simeq\operatorname{End}_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}(p_{T*}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}),

under which
(1,w)
corresponds to
w
. We also view
\delta_{w}
as an element in the left hand side.
For any
w\in W
,
\{(1,w^{\prime})\}_{w^{\prime}<w}
and
\{\delta_{w^{\prime}}\}_{w^{\prime}<w}
generate the same
k(T_{\sigma})
-subspace in the vector space

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma}),{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}))\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}k(T_{\sigma})

by definition of
\delta_{w}
.
Thus we have
F^{\geq w}\subset{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\sslash W^{\sigma})}{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
is
\bigcap_{w^{\prime}<w}\ker(\delta_{w^{\prime}})
, which is free as
\{\delta_{w}\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
forms a basis of
\operatorname{End}_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}(p_{T*}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})
by Lemma
2.5.5
.
Other submodules or quotient modules of
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})
in the statement are free for the same reason.

Moreover, for
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}
,
\{(L\delta)_{w^{\prime}}\}_{w^{\prime}<w}
and
\{\delta_{w^{\prime}}\}_{w^{\prime}<w}
generate the same
k(T_{\sigma})
-subspace in the vector space

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma}),{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}))\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}k(T_{\sigma})=\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}},{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}k(T_{\sigma})

by definition of
(L\delta)_{w}
.
As the filtration of
\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B})
is defined by saturation, we have
F^{\geq w}\subset\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})
is
\bigcap_{w^{\prime}<w}\ker((L\delta)_{w^{\prime}})
, which is free as
\{(L\delta)_{w}\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
forms a basis of the module

\operatorname{End}(\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}(\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}),{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})).

Other submodules or quotient modules of
\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})
in the statement are free for the same reason.

For the statement about the generator of
F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}
, note that
\delta_{w}^{*}
is its generator the coefficient of
\delta_{w}
at
w
is
L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}{\mathfrak{n}}})
and other terms are in the places that are smaller than
w
in the Bruhat order. Thus
\delta_{w}^{*}-L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}{\mathfrak{n}}})^{-1}e_{w}
lies in
F^{>w}
, proving the case for
\operatorname{eis}
. As the leader term of
(L\delta)_{w}
is
L_{w}L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}{\mathfrak{n}}})
up to a unit, the statement for
\operatorname{Eis}
follows similarly.
∎

By Lemma
2.5.12
,
1\mapsto L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}{\mathfrak{n}}})L_{w}e_{w}
defines an isomorphism of functors

w^{*}\simeq F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}(\operatorname{CT}_{B^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B})

as an analog of a classical property of the Mackey filtration.

Now we can prove the main theorem of this subsection, calculating the composition of the inclusion of
\mathbb{1}
in
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}/F^{>1}
and the adjoint of the intertwining operator.

Theorem 2.5.13
.

Let
\chi
be a character in
X^{*}(T^{\sigma})^{W^{\sigma}}
.
Suppose that there is an other Borel subgroup
B^{\prime}
in
G
stable under
\sigma
and containing
T
.
The set of natural transformation of functors over the category
D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma})_{\chi}
, viewed as an
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})=\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{1})
-module acting on the source
(or equivalently on the target)

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}/F^{>1})

is free and of rank
1
. Moreover, there exists a generator of this module such that the composition

\mathbb{1}\to\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}/F^{>1}\to\mathbb{1}

is the multiplication by
L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{B}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{B^{\prime}}})^{-1}
,
where the morphism

\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{1}

is given by the adjunction of the intertwining operator
j_{B|B^{\prime}}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Eis}_{B}
.

Proof.

We firstly consider the case for
\chi=1
.
There is a unique
w\in W^{\sigma}
such that
B^{\prime}=w^{-1}(B)
.
As
{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}
is a generator of the category
D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma})_{1}
and
\operatorname{End}({\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
,

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}/F^{>1})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{b}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}),\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})/F^{>1})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{b}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w},\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})/F^{>w}),

where the “
\operatorname{Hom}_{b}
” here denotes the bimodule homomorphism space and the second isomorphism is given by twisting by
w
.

Recall that we may identify the

k(T_{\sigma})
-vector spaces

{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}k(T_{\sigma})=\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}k(T_{\sigma})

and the sets
\{(1,w)\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
,
\{\delta_{w}\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
and

\{(L\delta)_{w}\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
are bases of its dual, in which lie
the
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-modules

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}},{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})\simeq\operatorname{End}(\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})\ \text{and}\ \operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma}),{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}))\simeq\operatorname{End}(\operatorname{eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}).

There is a splitting of the
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-bimodule

{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma}\times_{T\sigma\sslash^{\prime}N_{G}(T\sigma)}T_{\sigma})\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})}k(T_{\sigma})\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in W^{\sigma}}k(T_{\sigma})_{w}e_{w}

such that
\{e_{w}\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
is the dual basis of
\{1,w\}_{w\in W^{\sigma}}
.

By Lemma
2.5.12
, the subquotients of the filtration
F^{>w}
are free. Thus

\operatorname{Hom}_{b}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w},\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})/F^{>w})\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{b}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w},\bigoplus_{w^{\prime}\in W^{\sigma}}k(T_{\sigma})_{w^{\prime}}e_{w^{\prime}}/F^{>w})\simeq k(T_{\sigma})e_{w}

given by taking its value at
1
. Moreover, by construction of the intertwining operator in
2.4.1
,
the adjunction of
j_{B|B^{\prime}}
,
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}}\to{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w}

is given by
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{B}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{B^{\prime}}})^{-1}(1,w)=L_{w}(1,w)
.
By Lemma
2.5.12
,
L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}({\mathfrak{n}})})^{-1}L_{w}^{-1}e_{w}
is a generator of the free
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-module
F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}
and
F^{\geq w}
is saturated in the module
\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})
.
Thus the homomorphism given by
1\mapsto L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}/w^{-1}({\mathfrak{n}})})^{-1}L_{w}^{-1}e_{w}

is a generator of the free
{\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})
-module
\operatorname{Hom}_{b}({\mathcal{O}}(T_{\sigma})_{w},\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B}{\mathcal{O}}_{T_{\sigma}})/F^{>w})
and we conclude the
\chi=1
case.

Then we consider the case for general
\chi
. Recall that tensoring by
{\mathcal{O}}_{\chi}
defines an equivalence of categories

D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma})_{1}\simeq D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathbb{L}}_{T\sigma})_{\chi}.

Recall that the construction
\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}
is compatible with this equivalence by Proposition
2.5.8
. The filtration
F^{>1}
is compatible with this equivalence by definition. The integral intertwining operator
j_{B^{\prime}|B}
is compatible with this equivalence by its construction in the proof of Proposition
2.4.1
.
Thus tensoring the generator of
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}/F^{>1})_{1}
defined above by
{\mathcal{O}}_{\chi}
defines the desired generator of
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{B}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}/F^{>1})_{\chi}
,
where the subscripts mean the
\operatorname{Hom}
-modules over the corresponding block.
∎

Imitating the construction of the classical unnormalized intertwining operator,
we divide the factor in Theorem
2.5.13
to obtain the unnormalized spectral intertwining operator.

Definition 2.5.14
.

The unnormalized intertwining operator
J_{B^{\prime}|B}
is defined as

L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{B^{\prime}}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{B}})j_{B^{\prime}|B},

viewing as a transformation
\operatorname{Eis}_{B}^{rs}\to\operatorname{Eis}_{B^{\prime}}^{rs}
.

We deduce Theorem
1.2.3
in the unipotent case.

3
The general case

In this section, we construct the spectral intertwining operators for some components of
\operatorname{Par}_{G}
, generalizing the results on the unipotent block.

3.1
Structure of general components

Firstly we describe the properties of the general connected components of the moduli stack of L-parameters. We will show that any connected component is isomorphic to the unipotent component of another group, quotiented by a finite group.

Let
F
be a
p
-adic local field. Let
W_{F}
be its Weil group and
I=I_{F}
be the inertia subgroup of
W_{F}
. Denote by
\mathrm{Fr}
the
arithmetic
Frobenius in
W_{F}
.

Let
G
be a reductive group over the algebraically closed field
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
(
\ell\neq p
) with
W_{F}
-action preserving a pinning. Note that it
comes from the Langlands dual of some
p
-adic reductive group
(Attention: we denote by
G
the group on the spectral side, instead of
G^{\vee}
).
Let
Z^{1}(W_{F},G)
be the moduli space of
\ell
-adically continuous 1-cocycles. By
[
5
]
, Proposition VIII.2.5, we may view this space as the moduli space of Weil–Deligne L-parameters, i.e., space of the pairs
(\phi,N)
, where
\phi
is a continuous 1-cocycle in discrete topology and
N\in{\mathcal{N}}_{G}
is nilpotent (called the monodromy part), satisfying
\mathrm{Ad}(\phi(w))N=q^{|w|}N
for all
w\in W_{F}

(
|\cdot|\colon W_{F}\to W_{F}/I_{F}\simeq\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathrm{Fr}\mapsto 1
).
We will denote by
Z^{1}(W_{F},G)_{0}
the moduli space of continuous 1-cocycle in discrete topology and we have a projection
Z^{1}(W_{F},G)\to Z^{1}(W_{F},G)_{0}

by ignoring the monodromy part.

Let
M
be a Levi subgroup of
G
stable under the
W_{F}
-action and

\phi\in Z^{1}(W_{F},M)
be an elliptic semisimple parameter. Let
P
be a parabolic subgroup of
G
stable under
W_{F}
with Levi subgroup
M
.

Denote by
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
the connected component of
\operatorname{Par}_{G}:=Z^{1}(W_{F},G)/G
containing
\phi
and denote by
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi,0}
the connected component of
\operatorname{Par}_{G,0}:=Z^{1}(W_{F},G)_{0}/G
containing
\phi
. Similarly for
P
and
M
. We will denote by
{}^{L}(-)
the L-group

(-)\rtimes W_{F}
for a subgroup of
G
stable under
W_{F}
.
Then
\phi\in Z^{1}(W_{F},M)
corresponds to a continuous group homomorphism
{}^{L}\phi\colon W_{F}\to{}^{L}M
.

The spectral Eisenstein series operator in general is defined as follows.

Definition 3.1.1

(Spectral Eisenstein series)
.

Notations as above, define

p_{P}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{P}\to\operatorname{Par}_{G}
induced by the inclusion and

q_{P}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{P}\to\operatorname{Par}_{M}
induced by the projection. The spectral Eisenstein operator
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
is defined as

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}:=p_{P*}\circ q_{P}^{*}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G}).

Similarly, we define
p_{P,0}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{P,0}\to\operatorname{Par}_{G,0}
and
q_{P,0}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{P,0}\to\operatorname{Par}_{M,0}
.
Then
\operatorname{eis}_{P}
is defined as

\operatorname{eis}_{P}:=p_{P,0*}\circ q_{P,0}^{*}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G}).

We will also consider the morphism and operators over the connected components
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
,
\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}
and
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
and use the same notations if there is no ambiguity, such as

p_{P}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}\to\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
,
q_{P}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}\to\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
and

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}:=p_{P*}\circ q_{P}^{*}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}).

We reduce to the case for the principal block by the following facts that any connected component of
Z^{1}(W_{F},G)
is similar to the unramified component of some group. Some of these facts are mentioned in a more general way in
[
3
]
.

In the following statements, we will consider the centralizer of a parameter such as
C_{G}(\phi|_{I})
, which always means the centralizer of the corresponding group homomorphism, such as
C_{G}({}^{L}\phi|_{I})
.

Lemma 3.1.2
.

(
[
3
]
, Theorem 3.12)

After an unramified twist of
\phi
, there is a pinning of
C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
preserved by the adjoint action of
{}^{L}\phi(\mathrm{Fr})

(such action is well-defined as
I
is normal in
W_{F}
).

Lemma 3.1.3
.

The connected component of the (twisted) centralizer of
\phi|_{I}
(i.e., the restriction of
\phi
to the inertia subgroup
I_{F}
) in
M
,
C_{M}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is a torus. Moreover,
C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is a reductive group and
C_{P}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is a Borel subgroup in it.

Proof.

As
I
is a normal subgroup of
W_{F}
,
\phi|_{I}
is also semisimple.
Thus we conclude that
C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is a reductive group,
C_{P}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is a parabolic subgroup in it and
C_{M}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is a corresponding Levi subgroup in it. Hence it suffices to prove that
C_{M}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is a torus.

By Lemma
3.1.2
, we may twist
\phi
such that
\sigma=\mathrm{Ad}({}^{L}\phi(\mathrm{Fr}))
preserves a pinning of
C_{M}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
. Moreover, the fixed points of
C_{M}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
under
\sigma
is
C_{M}(\phi)
.
As
\phi
is elliptic in
M
, the group
C_{M}(\phi)/Z_{M}
is finite. We may replace
M
by its quotient
M/Z_{M}
. Then the centralizer
C_{M}(\phi)
is finite.
Suppose that
C_{M}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
is not a torus. We have that
\sigma
permutes a pinning
\{X_{\alpha}\}
of it.
Thus
\sigma
fixes the vector
0\neq X:=\sum_{\alpha\in O}X_{\alpha}
for any orbit
O
of simple roots of
C_{M}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
under the
\sigma
-action and it will fix the one-parameter subgroup
\exp(tX)
, a contradiction.
∎

From now on, we assume that
\phi
satisfies that the adjoint action of
{}^{L}\phi(\mathrm{Fr})
on
C_{G}(\phi|_{I})^{\circ}
fixes a pinning of the Borel pair
(C_{P}(\phi|_{I})^{\circ},C_{M}(\phi|_{I})^{\circ})
, by replacing it by an unramified twist. We will denote the adjoint action of
{}^{L}\phi(\mathrm{Fr})
by
\sigma
. Note that these constructions are independent of the choice of
\mathrm{Fr}
.

Proposition 3.1.4
.

The connected component
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
is isomorphic to
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})\sigma}/C_{G}^{1}(\phi|_{I})
, where
C_{G}^{1}(\phi|_{I})
is the open and closed subgroup of
C_{G}(\phi|_{I})
that stabilizes
C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})
under the
\sigma
-twisted conjugation (equivalently saying,
\pi_{0}(C_{G}^{1}(\phi|_{I}))=\pi_{0}(C_{G}(\phi|_{I}))^{\sigma}
).
Similarly for
P
and
M
.
Moreover we have
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi,0}\simeq C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})\sigma/C_{G}^{1}(\phi|_{I})
.

More precisely, the isomorphism is induced by

\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})\sigma}\to\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi},\quad(g\sigma,N)\mapsto(\chi_{g}\phi_{0},N)

where
\chi_{g}
denotes the unipotent parameter
W_{F}\to C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})\sigma
sending
\mathrm{Fr}
to
g
.

Proof.

We view
\operatorname{Par}_{G}
as the moduli space of Weil–Deligne L-parameters
\operatorname{Par}_{G}^{\text{WD}}
.
As
I_{F}
is profinite,
(\phi_{1},N_{1})
,
(\phi_{2},N_{2})
are in the same connected component only if
\phi_{1}|_{I}
and
\phi_{2}|_{I}
are
G
-conjugate. Thus we have

\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}\subset G\times^{C_{G}(\phi|_{I})}\{(\phi^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Par}_{G}^{\text{WD}}\mid\phi^{\prime}|_{I}=\phi|_{I}\}.

We have an isomorphism of schemes (compare the arguments in
[
3
]
, Section 3.1)

\{(\phi_{0},N)\mid\phi_{0}\in Z^{1}_{\mathrm{Ad}(\phi)}(W_{F}/I_{F},C_{G}(\phi|_{I})),N\in{\mathcal{N}}_{C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})},\mathrm{Ad}(\phi_{0}(w))N=q^{|w|}N\}\simeq\{(\phi^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Par}_{G}^{\text{WD}}\mid\phi^{\prime}|_{I}=\phi|_{I}\},

via
(\phi_{0},N)\mapsto(\phi_{0}\phi,N)
, where
\mathrm{Ad}(\phi|_{I})
defines a
W_{F}/I_{F}
-action on
C_{G}(I_{F})
.

Thus the connected component of
\{(\phi^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Par}_{G}^{\text{WD}}\mid\phi^{\prime}|_{I}=\phi|_{I}\}
containing
\phi|_{I}
is

\{(\phi_{0},N)\mid\phi_{0}\in Z^{1}_{\mathrm{Ad}(\phi)}(W_{F}/I_{F},C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})),N\in{\mathcal{N}}_{C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})},\mathrm{Ad}(\phi_{0}(w))N=q^{|w|}N\},

which is exactly
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{C_{G}^{\circ}(\phi|_{I})\sigma}
. Thus we conclude. The statement for
P
,
M
and
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi,0}
, etc. follows similarly.
∎

From now on, we will denote by
M_{\phi}:=C_{M}(\phi|_{I})
,

P_{\phi}:=C_{P}(\phi|_{I})
,
P^{\prime}_{\phi}:=C_{P^{\prime}}(\phi|_{I})
and

G_{\phi}:=C_{G}(\phi|_{I})
and similarly for the groups with superscripts
\circ
and
1
. We have the following corollary.

Corollary 3.1.5
.

The morphisms
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}\leftarrow\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}\to\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
are isomorphic to

\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}\leftarrow\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma}/P_{\phi}^{1}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{1},

where the morphisms are induced by the natural morphisms
M_{\phi}^{1}\leftarrow P^{1}_{\phi}\to G^{1}_{\phi}
and morphisms between their connected components.

Moreover, denote by

\overline{q_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\colon\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/P^{1}_{\phi}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M^{1}_{\phi},

\overline{p_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\colon\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/P^{1}_{\phi}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M^{1}_{\phi}G_{\phi}^{\circ},

and the projection

\pi_{\phi}\colon\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M^{1}_{\phi}G_{\phi}^{\circ}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G^{1}_{\phi}.

Then the Eisenstein operator
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}=p_{P*}q^{*}_{P}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi})

can be identified with
\pi_{\phi*}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
,
where

\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}:=\overline{p_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{q_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
.
Similarly, we have
\operatorname{eis}_{P}\simeq\pi_{\phi,0*}\circ\overline{\operatorname{eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
where the operators are naturally defined.

As
M_{\phi}^{\circ}
is a torus,
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}=M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
.
In particular,
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi,0}=\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
and the source of
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
and
\operatorname{eis}_{P}
are identical.

Next we analyze the structure of the groups
M_{\phi}
,
P_{\phi}
and
G_{\phi}
.

Lemma 3.1.6
.

The maps between connected components satisfy that
\pi_{0}(M^{1}_{\phi})\simeq\pi_{0}(P^{1}_{\phi})
and
\pi_{0}(P^{1}_{\phi})\to\pi_{0}(G^{1}_{\phi})
is injective.

Proof.

The first argument follows from the fact that
M
is a deformation retract of
P
. For the second argument, it suffices to prove that

\pi_{0}(P_{\phi})\to\pi_{0}(G_{\phi})
is injective.
Note that
P_{\phi}
normalizes
P_{\phi}
in
G_{\phi}
. Thus we have that
P_{\phi}\cap G_{\phi}^{\circ}
normalizes
P_{\phi}^{\circ}
in
G_{\phi}^{\circ}
. As
P^{\circ}_{\phi}
is a Borel subgroup of
G_{\phi}^{\circ}
, the normalizer of
P_{\phi}^{\circ}
in
G_{\phi}^{\circ}
is itself. Thus
P_{\phi}\cap G_{\phi}^{\circ}=P_{\phi}^{\circ}
and we conclude.
∎

To analyze the structure of
G_{\phi}^{1}
, we define the following notion of “normalizer”.

Definition 3.1.7

(Normalizer of
(M,\phi)
)
.

Let
N_{G}(M,\phi)
be the subgroup of
G
defined as

\{g\in N_{G}({}^{L}M)\mid\mathrm{Ad}(g)\phi\in X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi\}.

Here
X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
, viewed as a subscheme of
Z^{1}(W_{F},M)
denotes the unramified twists of
\phi
by characters
W_{F}/I\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}
.

By definition we have

N_{G}(M,\phi)\cap M=M^{1}_{\phi}
and
N_{G}(M,\phi)\subset G^{1}_{\phi}
.
In particular,
M_{\phi}^{1}
is a normal subgroup of
N_{G}(M,\phi)
of finite index.
We have another interpretation of the normalizer of
(M,\phi)
.

Lemma 3.1.8
.

The subgroup
N_{G}(M,\phi)
in
G
can be identified with the subgroup

N_{G_{\phi}^{1}}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)=\{g\in G\mid\mathrm{Ad}(g)(X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi)\subset X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi\}\subset G.

Note that these two groups are equal under the identification

M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\operatorname{\xrightarrow{\sim}}X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi,\quad m\sigma\mapsto\chi_{m}\phi,

where
\chi_{m}
is the unipotent parameter
W_{F}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}
sending
\mathrm{Fr}
to
m
.

We remark that the condition
\mathrm{Ad}(g)(X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi)\subset X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Ad}(g)(X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi)=X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
, as
\mathrm{Ad}(g)
defines an automorphism of
X_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
.

Proof.

For any
g\in N_{G}(M,\phi)
and any
\phi^{\prime}\in X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
, the image of
\mathrm{Ad}(g)(\phi^{\prime})
is in
M
. Moreover,
\mathrm{Ad}(g)\phi|_{I}=\phi|_{I}
and thus
\mathrm{Ad}(g)(X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi)\subset X_{M_{\phi}}\phi
. Thus
\mathrm{Ad}(g)(X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi)\subset X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
by connectivity. Hence
N_{G}(M,\phi)\subset N_{G_{\phi}^{1}}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)
.

It remains to prove that
N_{G_{\phi}^{1}}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)\subset N_{G}(M,\phi)
. For
g\in N_{G_{\phi}^{1}}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)
, the requirement
\mathrm{Ad}(g)\phi\in X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
is trivial.
Then we prove the statement
g\in N_{G}({}^{L}M)
. By definition
g
normalizes the subgroup

M_{\phi}^{\circ}\operatorname{im}({}^{L}\phi)\subset{}^{L}G.

This subgroup is generated by
\operatorname{im}({}^{L}(\phi|_{I}))
,
\sigma={}^{L}\phi(\mathrm{Fr})
and
M_{\phi}^{\circ}
. We claim that its center is
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
. Indeed, we have an exact sequence

1\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\operatorname{im}({}^{L}\phi)\to W_{F}\to 1

and the center of
W_{F}
is trivial. Then the center of
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\operatorname{im}({}^{L}\phi)
is contained in
M_{\phi}^{\circ}
and then in
M_{\phi}^{\circ}
as it centralizes
{}^{L}\phi(\mathrm{Fr})
.

Hence
g
normalizes the connected component
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
of the center. As
\phi\in Z^{1}(W_{F},M)
is elliptic, we have

(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}=(Z(M)^{W_{F}})^{\circ}.

Thus
g
normalizes the centralizer

C_{{}^{L}G}((Z(M)^{W_{F}})^{\circ})\simeq{}^{L}M

where this identity follows from
[
2
]
, Lemma 3.5 and we conclude.
∎

Lemma 3.1.9

(Structure of
G_{\phi}^{1}
)
.

The group
G_{\phi}^{1}
can be written as the product
G_{\phi}^{\circ}N_{G}(M,\phi)
.

Proof.

Consider the semidirect product
G_{\phi}^{0}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
.
For an element
g\in G_{\phi}^{1}
,
\mathrm{Ad}(g)
induces an automorphism of
G_{\phi}^{\circ}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
. Thus in this group it sends the minimal Levi subgroup
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
to a minimal Levi subgroup. Here the Levi subgroups are in the sense of
[
2
]
, 3.4. Hence there exists
g_{0}\in G_{\phi}^{\circ}
satisfying

\mathrm{Ad}(g_{0}g)(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle)\subset M_{\phi}^{\circ}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle

and then
g_{0}g\in N_{G}(M,\phi)
by Lemma
3.1.8
.
∎

Proposition 3.1.10

(Structure of
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
)
.

N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{\circ}
can be expressed as a semidirect product

N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{\circ}\simeq\pi_{0}(G^{1}_{\phi})\ltimes N_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma)/M_{\phi}^{\circ},

such that the elements in
\pi_{0}(G_{\phi}^{1})
stabilize
P_{\phi}^{\circ}
.
Note that the second component is isomorphic to
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
, the
\sigma
-fixed points of the Weyl group of
G^{\circ}_{\phi}
.

In particular, we have

N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}\simeq\pi_{0}(G^{1}_{\phi}/M^{1}_{\phi})\ltimes W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma},

Proof.

As
N_{G}(M,\phi)=N_{G_{\phi}^{1}}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)
by Lemma
3.1.8
, we have

(N_{G}(M,\phi)\cap G_{\phi}^{\circ})/M_{\phi}^{\circ}\simeq N_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)/M_{\phi}^{\circ}\simeq W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}.

By Lemma
3.1.9
,
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{\circ}
surjects to
\pi_{0}(G_{\phi}^{1})
. Thus it suffices to find a section as a group homomorphism of this surjection satisfying the condition about
P_{\phi}^{\circ}
.

For
\gamma\in N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{\circ}
, choose arbitrarily a preimage
\tilde{\gamma}\in N_{G}(M,\phi)
. Then the torus
M_{\phi}^{\circ}
is stable under the
\sigma
-twisted
\tilde{\gamma}
-conjugation on
G_{\phi}^{\circ}
and thus
\tilde{\gamma}(P^{\circ}_{\phi})\sigma(\tilde{\gamma})^{-1}
is a
\sigma
-stable standard Borel subgroup. This Borel subgroup is independent of the choice of
\tilde{\gamma}
and we will denote it simply by
\gamma(P^{\circ}_{\phi})\sigma(\gamma)^{-1}
.

There exists a unique element
\gamma_{0}
in
N_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma)/M_{\phi}^{\circ}\simeq W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
such that
\gamma_{0}\gamma P^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma(\gamma_{0}\gamma)^{-1}=P^{\circ}_{\phi}
. Thus
\gamma_{1}:=\gamma_{0}^{-1}\gamma
is the unique element in the coset
\gamma W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
such that
P_{\phi}^{\circ}
is stable under the
\sigma
-twisted
\gamma_{1}
-conjugation. Thus we obtain a section

\pi_{0}(G^{1}_{\phi})\to N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{\circ},\quad\gamma W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}\mapsto\gamma_{1}.

and the section is a group homomorphism by the uniqueness above.
∎

Lemma 3.1.11
.

The morphism
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
is a homeomorphism.
Moreover, the
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
-action on
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
is free over a dense open subset of it.

Proof.

The group
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
is a subgroup of finite index
in the stabilizer of the
M_{\phi}^{1}
-action of any point
x\in M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
.
Thus the isotropy groups of all points in

\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}
have the same dimension. Thus all orbits in
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}
are closed and the morphism from
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}
to its coarse moduli space induces a homeomorphism.

Consider the
N_{G}(M,\phi)
-action on
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\simeq X_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}\phi
, the subscheme of unramified twists of
\phi
. Suppose that
g\in N_{G}(M,\phi)
acts trivially on this scheme. As
g
fixes
\phi
,
g\in C_{G}({}^{L}\phi)
. For
m\in M_{\phi}^{\circ}
, denote by
\chi_{m}
the unipotent parameter
W_{F}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}
sending
\mathrm{Fr}
to
m
. As
g
fixes
\chi_{m}\phi
,
g\in C_{G}(m)
. Varying
m
, we obtain that
g
centralizes
M_{\phi}^{\circ}
. As
(Z(M)^{W_{F}})^{\circ}\subset M_{\phi}^{\circ}
, we have

C_{{}^{L}G}(M_{\phi}^{\circ})\subset C_{{}^{L}G}((Z(M)^{W_{F}})^{\circ})={}^{L}M

where the identity is from
[
2
]
, Lemma 3.5. Thus
g\in N_{G}(M,\phi)\cap M=M_{\phi}^{1}
.

We conclude that the
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
-action on
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
cannot factor through a proper quotient group. Thus the action is free over a dense open subset since
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
is a finite group.
∎

3.2
Intertwining over the regular semisimple and generic locus

We will define the regular semisimple locus and the generic locus in general and construct the normalized intertwining operator in this subsection. We retain the notations of the previous section.

Definition 3.2.1

(L-function)
.

For
s=0,1
and
\psi\in\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
, the L-function, as a rational function on
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
, is defined as

L(s,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})(\psi):=\det(1-q^{-s}{}^{L}\psi(\mathrm{Fr}),{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}^{\phi(I)})^{-1}.

As
{\mathfrak{n}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}={\mathfrak{n}}_{P}^{\phi(I)}
, we have
L(s,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})(\chi_{m}\phi)=L(s,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}})(m)
for any
m\in M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
(where
\chi_{m}
is the unramified
1
-cocycle corresponding to
m
). In particular, the
M^{1}_{\phi}
-action on
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
preserves the L-function
L(s,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}})
. Thus we may view the L-function as a rational function on
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}
.

Definition 3.2.2

(Regular semisimple locus and generic locus)
.

We define the regular semisimple or generic loci as follows:

•

For
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
, consider the cotangent complex
L\iota_{M,G}
of the morphism
\iota_{M,G}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}\to\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
induced by
M\to G
. Then the generic locus
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
is defined to be where
L^{1}\iota_{M,G}=0
and the regular and semisimple locus is defined to be where
L^{-1}\iota_{M,G}=0
.

•

For
\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}
, the regular semisimple or generic locus of it is defined to be the pullback of the corresponding locus of
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
along
q_{P}
.

•

For
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
, the regular semisimple or generic locus of it is defined to be the image of the corresponding locus of
\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}
along
p_{P}
, as it is so in the unipotent case.

Similarly we define the loci for
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi,0}
, etc. for the parameters without monodromy.

Note that the loci on
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
depends on the ambient group
G
. We will always take
G
as the ambient group in the definition of the loci.

We remark that the regular semisimple and generic locus
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}^{rs,g}\subset\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
here can be identified with the open substack of parameters of “Langlands-Shahidi type” in the sense of Zou (
[
20
]
) and also the open substack of “generous” L-parameters in the sense of Hansen
[
9
]
.

Lemma 3.2.3
.

The regular, semisimple or generic locus in
G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma

(resp.
P_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
,
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
) is invariant under the
G_{\phi}^{1}
(resp.
P_{\phi}^{1}
,
M_{\phi}^{1}
)-action.
Similarly for
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}
, etc.

Proof.

We prove the statement for
G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
and the statements for

P_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
and
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma

follow similarly.

For the statement about the regular locus, note that for
x\sigma\in G_{\phi}^{\circ}
, the stabilizers satisfy that
C_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(\mathrm{Ad}(g)(x\sigma))=\mathrm{Ad}(g)(C_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(x\sigma))
.
Recall that the regularity of
x
is defined by the dimension of its stabilizer.
Thus the regular locus is stable under its action.

For the statement about the semisimple locus, note that
x\sigma\in G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
is semisimple if and only if
\chi_{x}\phi
is semisimple as an L-parameter, where
\chi_{x}
denotes the
1
-cocycle on
W_{F}/I
corresponding to
x
.
As any conjugation of a semisimple L-parameter is again semisimple, the semisimple locus is stable under conjugation.

For the statement about the generic locus,
note that a semisimple element
x\sigma\in G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
is generic if and only if
(x\sigma,N)\in{\mathbb{L}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}
implies
N=0
. Thus the
G_{\phi}^{1}
-action preserves the semisimple and generic locus.
Moreover, recall that the genericity is defined via the twisted Chevalley morphism. Thus it is a condition on

G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash G_{\phi}^{\circ}
to which the
G_{\phi}^{1}
action be descended. As any element in
G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash G_{\phi}^{\circ}
has a semisimple representative, the generic locus is stable under conjugation.

The statements about
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}
hold as the loci are defined by pulling back the corresponding loci along the projection
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}\to G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma
.
Similarly for
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}
and
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}
.
∎

From the lemma above, we can identify these loci on
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
,

\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}
and
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
with the corresponding loci in the unipotent case.

Proposition 3.2.4
.

Under the identification in Proposition
3.1.4
, the regular semisimple locus or the generic locus of
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}

(resp.
\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}
,
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
) can be identified with
the corresponding locus of

\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M^{1}_{\phi}
(resp.
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/P^{1}_{\phi}
,
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G^{1}_{\phi}
).
Similarly for the parameters without monodromy, such as
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi,0}
, we define the regular semisimple locus.

In particular, the loci of
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
are open.

Proof.

The statement for
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
follows from Proposition
2.2.6
about the cotangent complex in the unipotent case. The statements
\operatorname{Par}_{P,\phi}
follows by definition. The statement for
\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}
holds since
{\mathbb{L}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{?}
is the image of
{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{?}
and the ?-locus is invariant under conjugation, where ? is
rs
,
g
or
rs,g
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.5
.

The morphisms of quotient stacks

(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}/N_{G}(M,\phi)\to(G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}/G^{1}_{\phi}.

and

\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{rs,g}/N_{G}(M,\phi)\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{rs,g}/G_{\phi}^{1}

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

We prove the statement without monodromy and the statement with monodromy follows directly.
It suffices to prove the statement after pulling back along

(G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}\to(G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}/G_{\phi}^{1},

under which the pullback of
(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}/N_{G}(M,\phi)
is

G_{\phi}^{1}\times^{N_{G}(M,\phi)}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}
.
By Lemma
2.2.10
, we have an isomorphism

G_{\phi}^{\circ}\times^{N_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma)}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}\simeq G_{\phi}^{\circ}.

Moreover, by Lemma
3.1.9
, we have

G_{\phi}^{\circ}N_{G}(M,\phi)=G_{\phi}^{1}\quad\text{and}\quad G_{\phi}^{\circ}\cap N_{G}(M,\phi)=N_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma)

Thus the morphism

G_{\phi}^{\circ}\times^{N_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\sigma)}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}\to G_{\phi}^{1}\times^{N_{G}(M,\phi)}(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs}

is an isomorphism and we conclude.
∎

We can generalize the notion of regular semisimple and generic locus on the entire
\operatorname{Par}_{M}
,
\operatorname{Par}_{P}
and
\operatorname{Par}_{G}
.

Definition 3.2.6

(Regular semisimple and generic locus in general)
.

Let
\psi\in\operatorname{Par}_{M}
be a semisimple parameter.
Suppose that
{}^{L}M_{0}\subset{}^{L}M
is a minimal Levi subgroup that contains
\psi
. Then
\psi
is elliptic in
M_{0}
and we define the regular semisimple and generic locus on
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\psi}^{rs,g}
to be the image
\iota_{M_{0},M}(\operatorname{Par}_{M_{0},\psi}^{rs,g})
under the morphism
\iota_{M_{0},M}\colon\operatorname{Par}_{M_{0},\psi}\to\operatorname{Par}_{M,\psi}
.
Note that for
\psi\in M
, all such
M_{0}
’s are
M
-conjugate.
Thus the locus
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\psi}^{rs,g}
is independent of the choice of
M_{0}
.
Moreover, by definition
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\psi}^{rs,g}\simeq{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\psi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{rs,g}/M_{\psi}^{1}
. Thus it is open.
Combining these loci for all
\psi
’s, we obtain an open substack
\operatorname{Par}_{M}^{rs,g}
for
\operatorname{Par}_{M}
.

By similar process, we define the locus
\operatorname{Par}_{G}^{rs,g}
for
G
. For
P
, the locus
\operatorname{Par}_{P}^{rs,g}
is defined to be the pullback
q_{P}^{-1}(\operatorname{Par}_{M}^{rs,g})
.

Lemma 3.2.7
.

The morphisms
\iota_{M,P}
(the morphism induced by the inclusion
M\subset P
) and
q_{P}
define an isomorphism
\operatorname{Par}_{M}^{rs,g}\simeq\operatorname{Par}_{P}^{rs,g}
. Moreover, the regular semisimple and generic loci for
P
and
G
are stable under
p_{P}
and
p_{P}^{-1}
.

Proof.

For both statements, it suffices to consider each connected component
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\psi}
, for semisimple parameters
\psi
. Choose a minimal Levi subgroup of
M
containing
\psi
.

For any
\psi^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Par}_{M,\psi}^{rs,g}
, choose a preimage
\psi^{\prime}_{0}
of it in
\operatorname{Par}_{M_{0},\psi}^{rs,g}
. By definition,
\iota_{M_{0},P}
and
\iota_{M_{0},M}
are étale at
\psi^{\prime}_{0}
. Thus
\iota_{M,P}
is étale at
\psi^{\prime}
. Hence
\iota_{M,P}
is étale on
\operatorname{Par}_{M}^{rs,g}
. Since
\iota_{M,P}
is the right inverse of
q_{P}
and
\operatorname{Par}_{P,\psi}
is connected,
\iota_{M,P}
and
q_{P}
are mutually inverse over the regular semisimple and generic locus.

As
\iota_{M,G}=p_{P}\circ\iota_{M,P}
,
p_{P}(\operatorname{Par}_{P,\psi}^{rs,g})=\operatorname{Par}_{G,\psi}^{rs,g}
. Moreover, for
\psi^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Par}_{G,\psi}^{rs,g}
, it is semisimple and without monodromy. Thus for any
\tilde{\psi}\in q_{P}^{-1}(\operatorname{Par}_{P,\psi})
,
it comes from
\operatorname{Par}_{M_{0},\psi}
. As
\iota_{M_{0},G}^{-1}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\psi}^{rs,g})=\operatorname{Par}_{M_{0},\psi}^{rs,g}
as in the unipotent case,
\tilde{\psi}\in\operatorname{Par}_{P,\psi}^{rs,g}
.
∎

Hence the loci are stable under
p_{P}
,
p_{P}^{-1}
,
q_{P}
and
q_{P}^{-1}
.
as in the unipotent case. We will use the superscript
rs
and
g
to denote the regular semisimple or generic locus respectively.
Then we define

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{?}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{?})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}^{?})

and

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M}^{rs,g})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G}^{rs,g})

by restricting
p_{P}
and
q_{P}
to the ?-loci, where ? is
rs
,
g
or
rs,g
. Similarly for
\operatorname{eis}_{P}^{?}
.

Now we define the normalized intertwining operator
J^{\circ}=J^{rs,g}
.
Recall that we have
\iota_{M,G}=p_{P}\circ\iota_{M,P}

and
\iota_{M,P}
is the inverse of
q_{P}
by Lemma
3.2.7
.
We obtain isomorphisms

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\simeq p_{P*}q_{P}^{*}\simeq p_{P*}(\iota_{M,P})_{*}\simeq(\iota_{M,G})_{*}.

Definition 3.2.8

(Intertwining over the regular semisimple and generic locus)
.

Let
Q
be an another parabolic subgroup of
G
stable under
W_{F}
such that
M\subset Q
is a Levi subgroup. Then we have Eisenstein operators

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M}^{rs,g})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G}^{rs,g}).

The intertwining operator

J_{Q|P}^{rs,g}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\operatorname{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g}

is defined as the composition

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\simeq(\iota_{M,G})_{*}\simeq\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g}

where the isomorphism
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\simeq(\iota_{M,G})_{*}
(similarly for
Q
) is constructed above. We construct
J_{Q|P,0}^{\circ}\colon\operatorname{eis}_{P}^{rs}\operatorname{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\operatorname{eis}_{Q}^{rs}
similarly.

As in the unipotent case, the intertwining operator
J^{\circ}_{Q|P}
generates all transformations in some sense.

Proposition 3.2.9
.

The intertwining operator

J^{rs,g}_{Q|P}=J_{Q|P}^{\circ}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\simeq\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g}

generates the free
{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M}^{rs,g}))
-module
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g})
.

Similarly,
J^{rs}_{Q|P,0}=J_{Q|P,0}^{\circ}
generates the free
{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,0}^{rs}))
-module
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{eis}_{P}^{rs},\operatorname{eis}_{Q}^{rs})
.

Proof.

We prove the statement for
\operatorname{Eis}
and the statement for
\operatorname{eis}
is similar.
As the intertwining operator
J^{\circ}_{Q|P}
is defined as
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\simeq(\iota_{M,G})_{*}\simeq\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g}
,
we have

\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g})\simeq\operatorname{End}((\iota_{M,G})_{*})

and
J^{\circ}_{Q|P}
corresponds to
1
in the right hand side
and it remains to show that

{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M}))\simeq\operatorname{End}((\iota_{M,G})_{*}).

We will then verify that
\iota_{M,G}
satisfies the conditions in Proposition
2.2.14
. It suffices to prove for each connected component
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\psi}
, where
\psi
is semisimple. Choose a minimal Levi subgroup
{}^{L}M_{0}\subset{}^{L}M
containing
\psi
.
By Lemma
3.2.5
,
\iota_{M,G}

can be identified with

\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{0,\psi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{rs,g}/N_{M}(M_{0},\psi)\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{0,\psi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{rs,g}/N_{G}(M_{0},\psi).

Thus
\iota_{M,G}
is an étale covering between connected algebraic stacks.

It remains to find a point in
(M_{0,\psi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs,g}
such that its stabilizers under the
M_{0,\psi}^{1}
-action and
N_{G}(M_{0},\psi)
-action are the same (and hence the its stabilizer under the
N_{M}(M_{0},\psi)
-action is also the same). In fact, by Lemma
3.1.11
,
there is an open and dense subset of
M_{0,\psi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{0,\psi}^{1}
such that
N_{G}(M_{0},\psi)
acts freely on it.
Then any point in the inverse image of this subset in
(M_{0,\psi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs,g}/M_{0,\psi}^{1}
satisfies this condition.
∎

From its construction,
J^{\circ}_{Q|P}
satisfies the transitivity,
proving the first main theorem
1.2.1
.

3.3
Block decomposition of
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})

Next we consider the block decomposition of
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
as in the unipotent case. We will only consider elliptic components and always identify
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
with
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}
.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

The morphism
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
is a
C_{M}(\phi)=(M_{\phi}^{1})^{\sigma}
-gerbe.
Moreover, the morphism
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
is a Galois covering and the gerbe is trivialized over this covering.

Proof.

By
[
3
]
, Theorem 6.10, the coarse moduli space of
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
is isomorphic to the Haines variety of
\phi
,
which consists of unramified central twists of
\phi
.
In particular, the morphism

X_{(Z(M)^{I})^{\circ}}\phi\to\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}

(where the left hand side denotes the unramified twists of
\phi
by an unramified cocycle in
(Z(M)^{I})^{\circ}
)
is surjective.
Since the
Z(M)^{I}
-action by twisting and the
C_{M}(\phi)
-action by conjugation on
Z^{1}(W_{F},M)_{\phi}
(the scheme of
\psi
’s satisfying
\psi^{\prime}|_{I}=\phi|_{I}
) commutes,
the isotropy groups of any point in
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}

are identical.

As

\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}\simeq M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}\simeq(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{\circ})/\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1}),

the isotropy group of any point in
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
contains
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
as a subgroup and the corresponding quotient is the stabilizer of its image in
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}
of the
\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})
-action.
Since the stabilizers of all points in
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}
are identical, there is a subgroup
H_{\phi}
of
\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})
acting trivially on
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}
such that
\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})/H_{\phi}
acts freely on
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}
. This proves
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
is a Galois covering.

Then we prove the statements about the gerbe.
The morphism
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
can be factored as

((M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{\circ})/H_{\phi})/(\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})/H_{\phi})\to((M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}/H_{\phi})/(\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})/H_{\phi})\to(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}/(\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})/H_{\phi}).

Thus it is a gerbe as a composition of gerbes and it is a
C_{M}(\phi)
-gerbe as
C_{M}(\phi)
is the isotropy group of
1\sigma
in
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}
. Moreover, recall that by Lemma
2.2.15
, there is a section
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{\circ}
. Thus we have a section

(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{\circ}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}

of the gerbe
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
after base change along the Galois covering
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})_{\sigma}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
.
∎

Recall that for a
H
-gerbe
{\mathcal{X}}\to X

where
X
is a connected
k
-scheme and
H
is a (not necessarily connected) reductive group, we have a block decomposition

D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})=\bigoplus_{V\in\operatorname{Irr}(H)}D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V}

where the category with subscript
V
consists of sheaves
{\mathcal{F}}
satisfying that for any point
x
in
X
, the
H
-representation on the fiber
{\mathcal{F}}_{x}
is
V
-isotypic.
Moreover, if the gerbe is trivial:
{\mathcal{X}}\simeq BH\times X
, for any
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V}
, we have

{\mathcal{F}}\simeq V\boxtimes\mathcal{H}om(V\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X},{\mathcal{F}})

where the “
\mathcal{H}om
” is viewed as a sheaf on
X
.

Note that for any coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
on
X
, there is an
{\mathcal{O}}(X)
-action on it.
This action induces a ring homomorphism from
{\mathcal{O}}(X)
to the Bernstein center of
D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V}
for any
V\in\operatorname{Irr}(H)
.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}\to X
be as above. Suppose that
{\mathcal{X}}
and
X
are smooth and the gerbe is étale-locally trivial. Then the ring homomorphism

{\mathcal{O}}(X)\to{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V})

is an isomorphism, for any
V\in\operatorname{Irr}(H)
.

Proof.

An element in
{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V})
is given by a compatible family
\{a_{\mathcal{F}}\}_{{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V}}
, where
a_{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{F}})
. As
{\mathcal{X}}
is smooth and
k
is of characteristic
0
,
D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V}
is generated by locally free sheaves and thus
a_{\mathcal{F}}
is determined by those elements for locally free
{\mathcal{F}}
. For locally free
{\mathcal{F}}
,
{\mathcal{O}}(X)\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{F}})
is injective. Thus the homomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}(X)\to{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V})
is injective.

It remains to prove that the homomorphism is surjective.
Fix an element
a\in{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(X)_{V})
and a locally free
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})_{V}
. It suffices to prove that
a_{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(X)
.
Choose arbitrarily a closed point
x
in
X
. Consider the completion
{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge}
of
{\mathcal{F}}
along the fiber of
x
. As the gerbe is étale-locally trivial,
{\mathcal{X}}_{x}^{\wedge}\simeq BH\times X_{x}^{\wedge}
is a trivial gerbe and thus over
{\mathcal{X}}_{x}^{\wedge}
, there exists
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that

{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge}\simeq(V\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}^{\wedge})^{n}.

As
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{X}}(V\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X,x}^{\wedge})={\mathcal{O}}(X_{x}^{\wedge})
,
a_{{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(X_{x}^{\wedge})
by compatibility. Here we view all those completions of sheaves as inverse systems in
D\operatorname{Coh}({\mathcal{X}})
.

We claim that the inverse image of
{\mathcal{O}}(X_{x}^{\wedge})
along the homomorphism

\operatorname{End}({\mathcal{F}})\to\operatorname{End}({\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge})

is
{\mathcal{O}}(X)
. We will denote by
(-)_{Y}
for a
X
-scheme
Y
the base change of
(-)
along
Y\to X
.
As the constructions
\operatorname{End}({\mathcal{F}}_{Y})
,
\operatorname{End}(({\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge})_{Y})
,
{\mathcal{O}}(Y)
and
{\mathcal{O}}(X_{x}^{\wedge})_{Y}
in the claim are sheafy over the étale site over
X
, it suffices to prove the claim after any surjective and étale base change.

Choose an étale covering
X^{\prime}\to X
trivializing the gerbe. Then choose a Zariski covering
\tilde{X}\to X^{\prime}
trivializing the sheaf

\mathcal{H}om(V\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X},{\mathcal{F}})
. Then
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{X}}\simeq(V\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{X}})^{m}
for some
m\in{\mathcal{N}}
over
{\mathcal{X}}_{\tilde{X}}
. Under this identification,

\operatorname{End}_{\tilde{X}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{X}})\simeq M_{m}({\mathcal{O}}(\tilde{X})).

Thus the inverse image of
{\mathcal{O}}(X_{x}^{\wedge})_{\tilde{X}}
is the scalar matrices, proving the claim. Hence
a_{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(X)
, proving the lemma.
∎

By Lemma
3.3.1
, the
C_{M}(\phi)
-gerbe
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
satisfies the conditions in Lemma
3.3.2
. Thus there is a block decomposition

D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})=\bigoplus_{V\in\operatorname{Irr}(C_{M}(\phi))}D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}

and

{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}))_{V}={\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})

for any
V
.
Restricting the gerbe
M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma/M_{\phi}^{1}\to M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma\sslash M_{\phi}^{1}
to the ?-locus, where ? is
rs
,
g
or
rs,g
, we have

{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{?}))_{V}={\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{?}).

We will use the subscript
V
, such as
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}^{?}_{P},\operatorname{Eis}^{?}_{Q})_{V}
to denote the
\operatorname{Hom}
-module over the subcategory
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
.

Decomposing Proposition
3.2.9
by block, we have the following corollary.

Corollary 3.3.3
.

Let
V
be an irreducible representation of
C_{M}(\phi)
.
The intertwining operator
J_{Q|P}^{\circ}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\simeq\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g}
generates the free
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{rs,g})
-module
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g})_{V}
. Similarly,
J_{Q|P,0}^{\circ}
generates the free
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{rs})
-module
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{eis}_{P}^{rs},\operatorname{eis}_{Q}^{rs})_{V}
.

3.4
Integral intertwining operators

Now we can prove the main theorem
1.2.2
about integral intertwining operators.

Theorem 3.4.1

(Integral intertwining operator)
.

Let
V
be an irreducible representation of
C_{M}(\phi)
such that the
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
-action on
V
is invariant under the Weyl group
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
.
After restricting the Eisenstein operators to the subcategory
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
of
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
,
there is a natural transformation
j_{Q|P}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\simeq\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}
, which is equal to
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}J^{\circ}_{Q|P}
after restricting to the regular semisimple and generic locus. Moreover,
j_{Q|P}
is a generator of
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q})_{V}
, as a free
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
-module of rank
1
.

Proof.

Recall that we have

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}=(\pi_{\phi})_{*}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}}

for

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1})\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{\circ}M_{\phi}^{1}),

\displaystyle\pi_{\phi}\colon\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{\circ}M_{\phi}^{1}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{1}.

We claim that
\pi_{\phi,*}
induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}},\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}})_{V}\operatorname{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q})_{V}.

In fact, for locally free
{\mathcal{F}}
, choose a point
x\in(M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma)^{rs,g}
satisfying that the isotropy groups of it and its image in
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{1}
are isomorphic (by Lemma
3.1.11
it is possible). Then by similar descent arguments as in the proof of Lemma
2.2.14
and Lemma
3.3.2
,
we have for any locally free sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1})_{V}
,

\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}{\mathcal{F}})\ \text{``}\cap\text{''}\ \operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge})=\pi_{\phi,*}(\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}}{\mathcal{F}},\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}}{\mathcal{F}})),

i.e., if there exists compatible
a_{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}{\mathcal{F}})
and
a_{{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge}}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge})
, then
a_{\mathcal{F}}
is in the image of
\pi_{\phi,*}
,
proving the claim.

Thus it suffices to prove similar statements for
\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}}
and
\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}}
.
The L-function

L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}=L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}})^{-1}

is invariant under the
\pi_{0}(M^{1}_{\phi})
-action and
J^{\circ}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}|P^{\circ}_{\phi}}
is the lifting of
J^{\circ}_{Q|P}
.
For
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
,
its lifting to
{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}
satisfies that its
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
-characters are invariant under the Weyl group
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
. Thus by Theorem
2.4.1
, the operator

j_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}|Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}=L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}})^{-1}J^{\circ}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}|P^{\circ}_{\phi}}

is well-defined.
Moreover, it is invariant by
\pi_{0}(M^{1}_{\phi})
and hence descends to an operator

j_{Q|P}
between
\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}}
and
\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}}
, which is
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}J^{\circ}_{Q|P}
after restricting to the regular semisimple and generic locus, as desired.

Next we prove the statement about the structure of
\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}},\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}})_{V}
. By Lemma
2.4.12
and descent, there is a natural inclusion

\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}},\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}})_{V}\subset\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}^{rs,g}},\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}^{rs,g}})_{V}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{rs,g})={\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}^{rs,g})^{\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})}

Thus it suffices to determine for what function
f\in{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{rs,g})
,
fJ^{\circ}_{Q|P}
can be extended to a well-defined operator
\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}}\to\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}}
.
Note that
fJ^{\circ}_{Q|P}
is well-defined if and only if its lifting
\tilde{f}J^{\circ}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}|P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\to\operatorname{Eis}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}
is well-defined, where
\tilde{f}
is its image in
By Theorem
2.4.1
, the statement is equivalent to

\tilde{f}\in{\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q^{\circ}_{\phi}}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P^{\circ}_{\phi}}})^{-1}

and then
f\in{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}
. Thus we conclude.
∎

Remark 3.4.2
.

The generalization of Proposition
2.3.1
about the generic locus holds by similar arguments as above.
More precisely, let
V
be an irreducible representation of
M_{\phi}^{1}
such that the
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
-action on
V
is trivial.
Then the intertwining operator

J^{\circ}_{Q|P}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs,g}\simeq\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs,g}
can be extended to an isomorphism
J^{g}_{Q|P}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{g}\simeq\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{g}
, which
generates the free
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{g})
-module
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{g},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{g})_{V}
.

Similarly,
J^{rs}_{Q|P,0}=J_{Q|P,0}^{\circ}
can be extended to an isomorphism

J_{Q|P,0}\colon\operatorname{eis}_{P}\simeq\operatorname{eis}_{Q}
, which
generates the free
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
-module
\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{eis}_{P},\operatorname{eis}_{Q})_{V}
.

3.5
Adjunction

Next we define the constant term functor
\operatorname{CT}_{P}
as the partial left adjoint functor of
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
, and finally construct the unnormalized intertwining operator. Note that we will always consider adjoint functors in the
\infty
-categorical sense.

Denote by
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
the direct sum of all
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
’s for irreducible representation
V
of
C_{M}(\phi)
such that
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
action is invariant under the Weyl group
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
.
Denote by
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi})_{M,W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
the thick subcategory generated by the image of

\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi}).

Note that the category
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi})_{M,W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
is independent of the choice of
P
: for
P^{\prime}
, there exists
w\in W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\circ}
such that

w(P_{\phi}^{\prime\circ})=P_{\phi}^{\circ}
and then
\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\prime}}=\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\circ w_{*}
.

The expected left adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
is denoted by

\operatorname{CT}_{P}\colon D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi})_{M,W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}.

We view
\operatorname{CT}_{P}{\mathcal{G}}
only formally as a functor
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}\to\mathsf{Ani}
for now. However, by Proposition
3.5.2
that we will prove later,
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}
for any
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
lies in
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
and thus

\operatorname{CT}_{P}
defines exactly a functor
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi})_{M,W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
.

Proposition 3.5.1

(Decomposition of
\operatorname{CT}\circ\operatorname{Eis}
)
.

There is a decomposition

\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\simeq\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\pi_{0}(G^{1}_{\phi}/M^{1}_{\phi})}\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma^{*},

where
\gamma
, being viewed as an element in
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
under the isomorphism in Proposition
3.1.10
,
means the
\gamma
-action on
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}
.
Here
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}

is the formal adjoint functor of
\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\simeq\pi_{\phi,*}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
. We have
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\simeq\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\pi_{\phi}^{*}
.
As
\pi_{\phi}\colon\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{\circ}M_{\phi}^{1}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{1}
is a Galois cover with Galois group
\pi_{0}(G_{\phi}^{1}/M_{\phi}^{1})
, we have

\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\simeq\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\pi_{\phi}^{*}\circ\pi_{\phi,*}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma^{*}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}},

where
\gamma
is the
\gamma
-action on
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{\circ}M_{\phi}^{1}
.
By Proposition
3.1.10
, the image of
\gamma
under the section
\pi_{0}(G_{\phi}^{1}/M_{\phi}^{1})\to N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}

stabilizes
P_{\phi}^{1}
.
Thus the
\gamma
-action restricts to actions on
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/P_{\phi}^{1}
and
\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}
.
Hence

\gamma^{*}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\simeq\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma^{*}

and we conclude.
∎

Proposition 3.5.2
.

For any element
{\mathcal{G}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G,\phi})_{M,W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
,
the formal
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}({\mathcal{G}})
is representable by an object in
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
.

In particular, after restricting the functor
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
to the block

D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
for
V
as above, its left adjoint is obtained by composing
\operatorname{CT}_{P}
with the functor of taking the
V
-isotypic component. The result will be denoted by
\operatorname{CT}_{P,V}
.

Moreover, for locally free
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
,
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}
is locally free.

Proof.

By similar formal arguments as in the end of the proof of Proposition

2.5.7
, it suffices to prove that for locally free

{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{1}
,
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}
is representable and locally free.

Fixing locally free
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{1}
, denote by
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
its lifting on
{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}}
. Then
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
is a
\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})
-equivariant sheaf.
The
(M_{\phi})^{\circ}
-action at any fiber of
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
is trivial by definition. Thus by Proposition
2.5.8
, the object
\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
exists and the
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
-action on any fiber of it is trivial.
As the construction
\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}

is functorial, we obtain a
\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})
-equivariant structure on

\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
and it can be descended to an object
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}}
in
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{1}
. This object satisfies that

\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})=\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}},\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}})^{\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})}=\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{(\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}{\mathcal{F}},\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}).

Thus it is the desired object
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}{\mathcal{F}}
. By Proposition
2.5.7
and
2.5.8
,
\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
is locally free. Hence
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}{\mathcal{F}}
is locally free.

As
\gamma^{*}{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{1}
and is locally free,
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma^{*}{\mathcal{F}}
is representable for any
\gamma\in\pi_{0}(G_{\phi}^{1}/M_{\phi}^{1})
. Thus
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}
is representable and locally free by the decomposition in Proposition
3.5.1
.
∎

Remark 3.5.3
.

As mentioned in remark
2.5.9
, over the category of ind-coherent sheaves, we may define a right adjoint
\operatorname{CT}_{P*}
of
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
even for general components of
\operatorname{Par}_{M}
and
\operatorname{Par}_{G}
. Moreover, the conjectural second adjunction implies that
\operatorname{CT}_{\bar{P}*}
is the totally defined left adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
.

Now we define a filtration of
\operatorname{CT}\circ\operatorname{Eis}
indexed by the “relative Weyl group”
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
.

Definition 3.5.4

(Order on
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
)
.

The partial order on
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
is defined as follows.
For
w,w^{\prime}\in N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
,

w\leq w^{\prime}\iff\overline{PwQ}\subset\overline{Pw^{\prime}Q}\subset G.

As we have a morphism of stacks
P_{\phi}^{1}\backslash G_{\phi}^{1}/Q_{\phi}^{1}\to P\backslash G/Q
,
for
w,w^{\prime}\in W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
and
\gamma\in\pi_{0}(G_{\phi}^{1}/M_{\phi}^{1})\hookrightarrow N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}

as in Proposition
3.1.10
,

\overline{P_{\phi}^{\circ}wQ_{\phi}^{\circ}}\subset\overline{P_{\phi}^{\circ}w^{\prime}Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}
implies that
\gamma w\leq\gamma w^{\prime}
.
Thus the restriction of the order on
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
over each coset
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}\gamma
refines the Bruhat order of
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
with respect to
(P_{\phi}^{\circ},Q_{\phi}^{\circ})
.

Lemma 3.5.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
and denote by
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
its lifting on
{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}
.
The filtration
\{F^{\geq w},F^{>w}\}
with respect to the order above of
\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\widetilde{\gamma^{*}{\mathcal{F}}}

indexed by
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
defined in Definition
2.5.10
can be uniquely descended to a functorial filtration
\{\bar{F}^{\geq w},\bar{F}^{>w}\}
of
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\gamma^{*}{\mathcal{F}}
.

Moreover, if
{\mathcal{F}}
is locally free, then all the subquotients on the filtration
\bar{F}
are locally free.

As in the unipotent case,
here we use the term “filtration” for a compatible family of functors, insisting the fact that it defines a classical filtration in an abelian category for locally free sheaves.

Proof.

We will omit
\gamma
in the notations, as only this summand is concerned.
As
{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}\to\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
is a Galois covering, the uniqueness part is clear.
It suffices to construct such a filtration for locally free
{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then the result extends by linearity.
Moreover, we can consider the case
P_{\phi}^{\circ}=Q_{\phi}^{\circ}
only
and the general case follows from multiplying the indices by some
w\in W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
.
Denote by
\operatorname{CT}^{rs,g}
the left adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}^{rs,g}
and
j^{rs,g}
the open immersion from the regular and semisimple locus.
Then
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}^{rs,g}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}}^{rs,g}}{\mathcal{F}}
exists and there is a natural inclusion

\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ j^{rs,g}_{*}\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}{\mathcal{F}}\subset\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}^{rs,g}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}}^{rs,g}}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}}

as it is so for
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
and descent.
Similar to Definition
3.2.8
,

\overline{\operatorname{Eis}^{rs,g}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
can be canonically identified with

\overline{\iota_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}}_{*}
, where

\overline{\iota_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\colon\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}^{rs,g}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}\to(\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}^{rs,g}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1})/W(G_{\phi})^{\sigma}\operatorname{\xrightarrow{\sim}}\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}^{rs,g}_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/G_{\phi}^{\circ}M_{\phi}^{1}.

As
\overline{\iota_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
is a Galois covering with Galois group
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
, we have

\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}^{rs,g}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}}^{rs,g}}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\overline{\iota_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}}^{*}\overline{\iota_{G_{\phi}^{\circ}}}_{*}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}}w^{*}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}}.

Then we have a filtration
F^{\geq w}
defined by
