\bar{F}^{\geq w}:=\bigcap_{w^{\prime}<w}\ker(\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}{\mathcal{F}}\hookrightarrow j^{rs,g}_{*}\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}^{rs,g}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}}^{rs,g}}{\mathcal{F}}\to j^{rs,g}_{*}w^{*}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}})

and similarly for
F^{>w}
. By constructions in Definition
2.5.10
,
\bar{F}
lifts to the filtration
F
of
\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
as desired.
Moreover, the subquotients of the filtration is locally free, as they are so after lifting to
{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}
.
∎

Definition 3.5.6

(Filtration of
\operatorname{CT}\circ\operatorname{Eis}
)
.

The filtration
\{F^{\geq w},F^{>w}\}
of the functor
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
on the category
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
indexed by
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
with respect to the above ordering is
defined as follows. Write
w=\gamma w_{0}
as in Proposition
3.1.10
. By Lemma
3.5.5
, there is a filtration
\bar{F}
of
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
. Then for locally free
{\mathcal{F}}
, as in the proof of Lemma
3.5.5
, we define

F^{\geq w}:=\bigcap_{w^{\prime}<w}\ker(\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}\hookrightarrow j^{rs,g}_{*}\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}{\mathcal{F}}\to j^{rs,g}_{*}w^{\prime*}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}})

and similarly for
F^{>w}
.
Note that the filtration is preserved under homomorphisms between locally free sheaves in
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
. We may extend by linearity to uniquely obtain a filtration on the functor
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
on
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
.

Moreover, let
V
be as above. By taking
V
-isotypic components, we obtain a filtration indexed by
N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
of the functor
\operatorname{CT}_{Q,V}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
on the category
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
. This filtration reduces to a
N_{G}(M,\phi,V)/M_{\phi}^{1}
-filtration, where the group
N_{G}(M,\phi,V)
is the subgroup of
N_{G}(M,\phi)
which containing those elements that preserve
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
under their action on
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
.

By definition, under the decomposition in Proposition
3.5.1
, if
w=\gamma w_{0}
for
w_{0}\in W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
,

F
restricts to the filtration
\bar{F}
on
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\gamma^{*}{\mathcal{F}}
.

Now we can prove the third main theorem
1.2.3
, about the unnormalized intertwining operator.

Theorem 3.5.7
.

Let
V
be an irreducible representation of
M_{\phi}^{1}
such that the
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
-action on
V
is invariant under the Weyl group
W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
.
The natural transformations between endofunctors on
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
,
viewed as an
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})={\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}))_{V}
-module

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q,V}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}/F^{>1})_{V}

is free and of rank
1
. Moreover, there exists a generator of the module such that the composition

\mathbb{1}\to\operatorname{CT}_{Q,V}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}/F^{>1}\to\mathbb{1}

is the multiplication by
L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}
,
where the morphism

\operatorname{CT}_{Q,V}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\to\mathbb{1}

is given by the adjunction of the intertwining operator
j_{Q|P}\colon\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\to\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}
defined in
3.4.1
.

Proof.

We may replace the functor
\operatorname{CT}_{Q,V}
by
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}
, as we have

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q,V}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}/F^{>1})_{V}\simeq\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}/F^{>1})

where in the latter
\operatorname{Hom}
, the terms are functors from
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
to
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{W^{\sigma}-\text{inv}}
and
\mathbb{1}
is viewed as the inclusion.

Firstly, we prove that for
\gamma\neq 1\in N_{G}(M,\phi)/M_{\phi}^{1}
,

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma/\bar{F}^{>1})=0.

As
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
and
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
are generated by locally free sheaves and by Lemma
3.5.5
, for locally free
{\mathcal{F}}
,
\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma^{*}{\mathcal{F}}/F^{>1}
is locally free. Thus we have an inclusion

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma/\bar{F}^{>1})\subset\operatorname{Hom}(j^{rs,g,*},\overline{\operatorname{CT}^{rs,g}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}^{rs,g}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\gamma^{*}j^{rs,g*}/\bar{F}^{>1}),

where
j^{rs,g}
is the open immersion from the regular semisimple and generic locus.
As in the proof of Lemma
3.5.5
, we have

\overline{\operatorname{CT}^{rs,g}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}^{rs,g}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\gamma^{*}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}}\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}}w^{*}\gamma^{*}j^{rs,g*}{\mathcal{F}}.

By Lemma
3.1.11
, there exists a point
x
in the coarse moduli of
\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}
such that
N_{G}(M,\phi)
acts freely on it.
Then for such
x
,

\operatorname{Hom}({\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge},w^{*}\gamma^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{x}^{\wedge})=0

and thus
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\gamma/\bar{F}^{>1})=0
.

Hence the inclusion map under the decomposition of
\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}
in Proposition
3.5.1

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}/\bar{F}^{>1})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}/F^{>1})

is an isomorphism. Furthermore, again from the decomposition of
\overline{\operatorname{CT}^{rs,g}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}^{rs,g}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}/\bar{F}^{>1})_{V}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(j^{rs,g*},\overline{\operatorname{CT}^{rs,g}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}^{rs,g}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}j^{rs,g*}/\bar{F}^{>1})_{V}=\operatorname{End}(j^{rs,g*})_{V}={\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi}^{rs,g})

under which
1
in the rightmost object corresponds in the second object to the canonical section of the adjoint of
J_{Q|P}^{\circ}
,
denoted by
s
. Thus it suffices to prove that
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}s
lies in the first
\operatorname{Hom}
-module and generates it as an
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
-module.

Consider the Galois covering
{\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}\to\tilde{{\mathbb{L}}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma}/M_{\phi}^{1}
and denoted by a tilde
the lifting of an object along the covering.
By definition,
\tilde{s}
is the canonical section of the adjoint of
J_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}|P_{\phi}^{\circ}}^{\circ}
.
Thus by Theorem
2.5.13
,
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}\tilde{s}
is a well-defined transformation

\mathbb{1}\to\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}/F^{>1}
. As the L-functions are invariant under the
\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})
-action,
the transformation

L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}s\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\overline{\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}}\circ\overline{\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}}/\bar{F}^{>1})_{V}

is well-defined.

Moreover, for
f\in{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})={\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})^{\pi_{0}(M_{\phi}^{1})}
,
choose arbitrarily a locally free sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}\in D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})_{V}
. Suppose that the transformation
fs
can be extended everywhere. Then

\widetilde{(fs)_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})}(\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}},\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}/F^{>1}).

We then prove the claim that the image of the module
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}/F^{>1})
in the
\operatorname{Hom}
-module for
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}
is saturated. As
{\mathcal{F}}
is locally free, it is a direct summand of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\chi}
’s, where
\chi
’s are characters of
(M_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
such that they are trivial on
(M_{\phi}^{\sigma})^{\circ}
. Hence it suffices to prove that the
{\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})
-submodule

{\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}/F^{>1})_{\chi}\subset\operatorname{Hom}({\mathcal{O}}_{\chi},\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\chi}/F^{>1})

is saturated. By Proposition
2.5.8
, it suffices to prove the case for
\chi=1
. By Lemma
2.5.12
and the proof of Theorem
3.5.1
, for
w\in W(G_{\phi}^{\circ})^{\sigma}
such that
Q_{\phi}^{\circ}=w(P_{\phi}^{\circ})
, the
{\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})
-module

\operatorname{Hom}({\mathcal{O}}_{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}/F^{>1})=\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}/F^{>1})=\Gamma(\operatorname{CT}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}/F^{>w})

is free and generated by
\{(L\delta)^{*}_{w^{\prime}}\}_{w^{\prime}\leq w}
and the submodule
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}/F^{>1})_{1}
corresponds to the submodule generated by
(L\delta)_{w}^{*}
, proving the claim.

Thus
\widetilde{(fs)_{\mathcal{F}}}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})
-multiple of a generator of
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{1},\operatorname{CT}_{Q_{\phi}^{\circ}}\operatorname{Eis}_{P_{\phi}^{\circ}}/F^{>1})
,
as it is a rational multiple. Hence
\tilde{f}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})
-multiple of
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}
as rational functions in
k({\mathbb{L}}_{M_{\phi}^{\circ}\sigma})
and then
f
is an
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
-multiple of
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}
as rational functions in
k(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
,
proving that
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}s
is a generator.
∎

From the proof, we have the following corollary as an analog of the Mackey’s formula for classical parabolic induction and restrictions.

Corollary 3.5.8
.

The descent of
L(1,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})L(0,\mathrm{Ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})^{-1}s
induces an isomorphism

\mathbb{1}\simeq F^{\geq 1}/F^{>1}(\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P})

and by twisting we generalize this results to all strata:

w^{*}\simeq F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}(\operatorname{CT}_{Q}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P}).

As in the unipotent case, we divide the integral intertwining operator to obtain the unnormalized intertwining operator.

Definition 3.5.9

(Unnormalized intertwining operator)
.

The unnormalized intertwining operator
J_{Q|P}
is defined to be the rational operator
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}\to\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}
by

J_{Q|P}:=L(0,\mathrm{ad}_{{\mathfrak{n}}_{Q}/{\mathfrak{n}}_{P}})j_{Q|P},

as a transformation
\operatorname{Eis}_{P}^{rs}\to\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}^{rs}
.

By Theorem
3.5.7
,
J_{Q|P}
is the desired unnormalized intertwining operator in Theorem
1.2.3
.

4
Compatibility

In this section, we will state the compatibility between the classical intertwining operator and the spectral Eisenstein operator defined above.

We first recall the definition of the classical intertwining operator.

Definition 4.0.1

(Classical intertwining operator)
.

Let
G
be a reductive group over a
p
-adic local field
F
,
P
,
Q
are two parabolic subgroups of
G
such that their Levi subgroups are the same, denoted
M
.
Then there are (normalized) parabolic induction functors

i_{P}^{G},i_{Q}^{G}\colon\mathsf{Rep}(M(F))\to\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))

and parabolic restriction functors

r_{P}^{G},r^{G}_{Q}\colon\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))\to\mathsf{Rep}(M(F)).

By Mackey’s formula, there exists a filtration
F^{\leq w}
of
r_{Q}^{G}\circ i_{P}^{G}

indexed by
w\in N_{G}(M)/M
and
F^{\leq 1}/F^{<1}\simeq\mathbb{1}
.
It is proved that the inclusion

\mathbb{1}\to r_{Q}^{G}\circ i_{P}^{G}/F^{<1}

has a unique
rational
section
r_{Q}^{G}\circ i_{P}^{G}/F^{<1}\to\mathbb{1}
.
The adjunction of

r_{Q}^{G}\circ i_{P}^{G}\to r_{Q}^{G}\circ i_{P}^{G}/F^{<1}\to\mathbb{1},

J_{Q|P}\colon i_{P}^{G}\to i_{Q}^{G}
is called the unnormalized intertwining operator.

From now, we always assume that
G
is a pure inner form
(G^{*})_{b}
for a quasi-split p-adic reductive group
G^{*}
and an element
b
in the Kottwitz set
B(G^{*})_{bas}
.
To state the compatibility result, we assume the following version of the categorical local Langlands correspondence.
Recall the definition and properties of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}}
, the moduli space of principal
G^{*}
-bundles over the Fargues–Fontaine curve in
[
5
]
, Chapter III and
D_{\text{lis}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}})^{\omega}
, the category of lisse étale sheaves over
\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}}
in
[
5
]
, Chapter V. In particular, there is an open immersion
i_{b}\colon*/G_{b}(F)\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, which induces an inclusion
i_{b!}\colon D\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))\to D_{\text{lis}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}})
.

Conjecture 4.0.2

(Categorical local Langlands correspondence)
.

Let
G=(G^{*})_{b}
be as above.
For a fixed Whittaker datum of
G^{*}
, there is an equivalence of categories

\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{G^{*}}\colon D_{\text{lis}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}})^{\omega}\simeq D\operatorname{Coh}^{\text{qc}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}}),

where
\omega
denotes the full subcategory of compact object and qc denotes the full subcategory of objects with compact supports,
satisfying the following properties:

1.

Under this functor, the parabolic induction operator corresponds to the Eisenstein operator. More precisely, let
P
be a parabolic subgroup of
G
with Levi subgroup
M
, denote by
\bar{P}
the opposite parabolic subgroup of
P
. We have
M=(M^{*})_{b}
for the quasi-split inner form
M^{*}
of
M
and the Whittaker datum for
G
induces a Whittaker datum for
M^{*}
. Then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathsf{Rep}(M(F)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\text{lis}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M^{*}})^{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\operatorname{Coh}^{\text{qc}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\text{lis}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}})^{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\operatorname{Coh}^{\text{qc}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{b!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{M^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{b!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{G^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\bar{P}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Eis}^{a}_{\bar{P}!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\vee}}}

commutes up to isomorphisms, where
\operatorname{Eis}^{a}_{\bar{P}!}
is the Eisenstein operator for
\operatorname{Bun}
’s, defined in
[
6
]
, Definition 2.1.8. In this notation, “
a
” stands for “automorphic side”.

2.

This functor is compatible with automorphisms: for an automorphism
\sigma
of
G^{*}
that preserves
b
and a pinning, it induces an isomorphism
\sigma^{\vee}
of
G^{\vee}
. Then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\text{lis}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}})^{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\operatorname{Coh}^{\text{qc}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\text{lis}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G^{*}})^{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\operatorname{Coh}^{\text{qc}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{b!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{G^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{b!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{G^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{\vee}}

commutes up to isomorphisms.
Such a functor is called categorical local Langlands correspondence.

3.

The correspondence is compatible with the Kottwitz isomorphism. More precisely, for any
V\in\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))
,
The
Z(G^{\vee})^{W_{F}}
-character of the fiber of
\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{G^{*}}\circ i_{b!}(V)
at any point in
\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}}
is
\kappa(b)
under the Kottwitz isomorphism
\kappa\colon B(G^{*})_{bas}\simeq X^{*}(Z(G^{\vee})^{W_{F}})
.

In particular, we obtain a fully faithful functor

\operatorname{LL}_{G}=\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{G^{*}}\circ i_{b!}\colon D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F)))\to D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}})

Moreover, since
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}})
is in the Bernstein center of the right hand side, it induces a homomorphism of rings

\operatorname{LL}_{G}^{*}\colon{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}})\to{\mathcal{Z}}(D\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))),

called the local Langlands correspondence for functions, which will satisfy similar compatibility conditions. The correspondence for functions induces for an irreducible representation
\pi
of
G(F)
a semisimple cocycle
\phi_{\pi}\in Z^{1}(W_{F},G^{\vee})
up to inner conjugation, called its semisimple L-parameter.

One possible way to construct such a categorical Langlands correspondence is in the article of Fargues–Scholze
[
5
]
. They defined the functor
\widetilde{\operatorname{LL}}_{G^{*}}
as the conjecture inverse of the functor in
[
5
]
, Conjecture X.1.4.
They also construct a correspondence for functions

{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}})\to{\mathcal{Z}}(D\mathsf{Rep}(G(F)))

in
[
5
]
, Corollary IX.0.3 (for general
G
). By their constructions, the categorical correspondence and the correspondence for functions are compatible. The assumption (1) holds for functions by
[
5
]
, Corollary IX.7.3 and it is conjectured to be true categorically in
[
8
]
, Conjecture 5.4.1. The assumption (2) holds for functions by
[
5
]
, Theorem IX.6.1 and it is natural to hope that is it true categorically. The assumption (3) is conjecture to be true in
[
9
]
, Conjecture 1.4.5 (it is also expected to be true classically in
[
12
]
, Conjecture F).

The conjectural categorical local Langlands correspondence induces an equivalence of blocks.

Proposition 4.0.3
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible, supercuspidal representation of
G(F)

such that its corresponding semisimple L-parameter
\phi_{\pi}\in\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}}
is elliptic. Denote by
\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi}
the block of the representation
\pi
(in which the irreducible objects are the unramified twists of
\pi
). Then there exists a unique irreducible representation
V_{\pi}
of
C_{G^{\vee}}(\phi_{\pi})
such that the categorical local Langlands correspondence
\operatorname{LL}_{G}
restricts to an equivalence of categories

D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi})\simeq D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})_{V_{\pi}}.

In particular, this equivalence induces an isomorphism of Bernstein centers

{\mathcal{O}}(\pi):={\mathcal{Z}}(D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi}))\simeq{\mathcal{Z}}(D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})_{V_{\pi}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}}),

where the last identity is from Lemma
3.3.1
and
3.3.2
.
Moreover, by assumption (3) of Conjecture
4.0.2
, the
Z(G^{\vee})^{W_{F}}
-character of
V_{\pi}
is
\kappa(b)
.

Proof.

The correspondence for functions induces a morphism of algebraic varieties, from the variety of unramified twists of
\pi
to the coarse moduli of
\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee}}
.
By connectedness, the image of this morphism lies in the coarse moduli of the connected component
\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}}
. Thus for any
V\in D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi})
, the support of
\operatorname{LL}_{G}(V)
is contained in
\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi}
.
As
\phi_{\pi}
is elliptic,
\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi}
is disjoint with the image of
\operatorname{Par}_{P^{\vee}}
for any proper parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
. Thus the image of
\operatorname{LL}_{G}(V)
under the (putative) right adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\vee}}
is
0
for any
V\in D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi})
.

Hence for any proper parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
,
\operatorname{CT}^{a}_{P*}i_{b!}(V)=0
, where
\operatorname{CT}_{*}
is the constant term operator defined in
[
6
]
, Definition 2.1.8, which it is the right adjoint of
\operatorname{Eis}^{a}_{P!}
. Thus

i_{b!}(D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi}))\subset D(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\mathrm{cusp}}

where the latter is defined by vanishing of all such
\operatorname{CT}_{P*}^{a}
’s and proved to be a direct summand of
D(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\mathrm{cusp}}
in
[
6
]
, Theorem 1.3.2. Again by
[
6
]
, Theorem 1.3.2,

i_{b!}(D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F)))\cap D(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\mathrm{cusp}}\subset D(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\mathrm{cusp}}

is a direct summand. As
D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi})
is a block of
D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F)))
,
i_{b!}(D(\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi}))
is a block of
D(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and it corresponds to a block of
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})
. By Lemma
3.3.1
,
\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}}
is a
C_{G^{\vee}}(\phi_{\pi})
-gerbe and thus there exists such a
V_{\pi}
.
∎

Moreover, we can prove that there is an isomorphism between the relative Weyl groups.

Proposition 4.0.4
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible, supercuspidal representation of
M(F)

such that its corresponding semisimple L-parameter
\phi_{\pi}\in\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee}}
is elliptic. Denote by
V_{\pi}
the representation of
C_{M^{\vee}}(\phi_{\pi})
as in Conjecture
4.0.3
.
Then under the isomorphism

N_{G(F)}(M(F))/M(F)\simeq N_{{}^{L}G}({}^{L}M)/{}^{L}M

of Weyl groups,
there is an isomorphism of relative Weyl groups

N_{G^{\vee}}(M^{\vee},\phi,V_{\pi})M^{\vee}/M^{\vee}\simeq N_{G}(M,\pi)/M

where the group
N_{G^{\vee}}(M^{\vee},\phi_{\pi},V_{\pi})
is defined in Definition
3.5.6
and the group
N_{G}(M,\pi)
is

\{g\in N_{G(F)}(M(F))\mid\pi\circ g\simeq\chi\pi,\chi\in X^{*}_{\text{ur}}(M(F))\},

such that their action on the isomorphic rings
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(\pi)
are compatible.

Proof.

For an element
w
in these isomorphic Weyl groups,
w
defines an automorphism of
M(F)
on the automorphic side and its corresponding automorphism of
{}^{L}M
on the spectral side. Hence by the compatibility of the categorical correspondence with isomorphisms,
\phi_{w(\pi)}=w(\phi_{\pi})
and
V_{w(\pi)}=w(V_{\pi})
.

By the equivalence of blocks
4.0.3
, these two representations
\pi,w(\pi)
are in the same block of
\mathsf{Rep}(M(F))
if and only if their corresponding L-parameters
\phi_{\pi}
and
\phi_{w(\pi)}
are in the same connected component and
V_{\pi}=V_{w(\pi)}
. Hence the relative Weyl groups are isomorphic.

By the compatibility of the geometric correspondence with isomorphisms, the action of the relative Weyl group on the isomorphic rings
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(\pi)
are compatible.
∎

Now we can state the compatibility theorem between classical and spectral intertwining operators. Let
\pi
be an irreducible, supercuspidal representation of
M(F)
such that its corresponding semisimple L-parameter
\phi_{\pi}\in\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee}}
is elliptic. By Proposition
4.0.3
, it corresponds to an irreducible representation
V_{\pi}
of
C_{M^{\vee}}(\phi_{\pi})
. Denote by
M^{*}
the quasisplit inner form of
M
. Note that for
b_{M}\in B(M^{*})_{bas}\simeq X^{*}(Z(M^{\vee})^{W_{F}})
corresponding to
M
, it comes from
b_{G}\in B(G^{*})_{bas}\simeq X^{*}(Z(G^{\vee})^{W_{F}})
. Thus
\kappa(b_{M})
is invariant under the Weyl group action. Moreover, since
\phi_{\pi}
is elliptic,
(Z(M^{\vee})^{W_{F}})^{\circ}=(C_{M^{\vee}}(\phi_{\pi}))^{\circ}
. Thus the representation
V_{\pi}
satisfies the condition in section 3.4 and 3.5 and we can define the intertwining operators over the category
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})_{V_{\pi}}
.

Theorem 4.0.5

(Compatibility)
.

Under the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(M(F))_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})_{V_{\pi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}(G(F))_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{G^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{LL}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\bar{P}}^{G},i_{\bar{Q}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Eis}_{P},\operatorname{Eis}_{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{LL}_{G}}

the intertwining operator satisfies the following compatibility:
for any
\pi_{0}\in\mathsf{Rep}(M(F))_{\pi}
,

\operatorname{LL}_{G}(J_{\bar{Q}|\bar{P}}\pi_{0})=J_{Q^{\vee}|P^{\vee}}(\operatorname{LL}_{M}\pi_{0})

up to a unit in
{\mathcal{O}}(\pi)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M,\phi})
.

Proof.

On the automorphic side, the intertwining operator is defined as the adjunction of the rational splitting of the inclusion of functors from
\mathsf{Rep}(M(F))_{\pi}
,

\mathbb{1}\to r_{\bar{Q}}^{G}\circ i_{\bar{P}}^{G}/F^{<1},

and by Theorem
3.5.7
, on the spectral side, the intertwining operator is the adjunction of the rational splitting of the following transformation of functors from
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})_{V_{\pi}}
,

\mathbb{1}\to\operatorname{CT}_{Q^{\vee}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\vee}}/F^{<1}.

By fully-faithfulness of the functor
\operatorname{LL}_{M}
and
\operatorname{LL}_{G}
,
\operatorname{CT}_{Q^{\vee}}
corresponds to
r_{\bar{Q}}^{G}
and thus

\operatorname{LL}_{M}(r_{\bar{Q}}^{G}\circ i_{\bar{P}}^{G}(\pi_{0}))\simeq\operatorname{CT}_{Q^{\vee}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\vee}}(\operatorname{LL}_{M}\pi_{0}).

As the inclusion in Theorem
3.5.7
is a generator of the
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi})
-module of rank
1
, it is unique up to a unit in
{\mathcal{O}}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi})
.

Moreover, after taking the direct summand of
r_{\bar{Q}}^{G}\circ i_{\bar{P}}^{G}
in the block
D(\mathsf{Rep}(M(F)))_{\pi}
and taking the direct summand of
\operatorname{CT}_{Q^{\vee}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\vee}}
in the block
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})_{V_{\pi}}
, their filtrations reduce to

N_{G^{\vee}}(M^{\vee},\phi,V_{\pi})M^{\vee}/M^{\vee}\simeq N_{G}(M,\pi)/M
-filtrations.
Thus it suffices to prove that the reduced filtrations correspond to each other under the isomorphic relative Weyl groups in Proposition
4.0.4
and it suffices to prove this statement for those
\pi_{0}
with locally free
\operatorname{LL}_{M}\pi_{0}
, as locally free sheaves in
D\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Par}_{M^{\vee},\phi_{\pi}})_{V_{\pi}}
generate this category.
For those
\pi_{0}
, the filtrations are “usual” filtrations in abelian categories.

Note that the order of the filtration are the same:
\overline{PwQ}\subset\overline{Pw^{\prime}Q}
if and only if

\overline{P^{\vee}wQ^{\vee}}\subset\overline{P^{\vee}w^{\prime}Q^{\vee}}
. Moreover, the filtrations satisfy that the subquotients are compatible:

•

The subquotient of
r_{\bar{Q}}^{G}\circ i_{\bar{P}}^{G}
at the stratum
w
satisfies
F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}\simeq w^{*}
by Mackey’s formula.

•

The subquotient of
\operatorname{CT}_{Q^{\vee}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\vee}}
, at the stratum
w
satisfies
F^{\geq w}/F^{>w}\simeq w^{*}
by Corollary
3.5.8
.

Hence by compatibility of the action of Weyl group, the filtrations on both sides of the formula

\operatorname{LL}_{M}((r_{\bar{Q}}^{G}\circ i_{\bar{P}}^{G}\pi_{0})_{\pi})\simeq(\operatorname{CT}_{Q^{\vee}}\circ\operatorname{Eis}_{P^{\vee}}(\operatorname{LL}_{M}(\pi_{0})))_{V_{\pi}}

(where the subscripts denotes the corresponding direct summands)
correspond to each other, as such a filtration is unique.
∎

By definition of the unnormalized intertwining operator on the spectral side (Definition
3.5.9
), we verify the Langlands conjecture
1.1.1
under the conjectural local Langlands correspondence, after changing the convention of the Frobenius as in Remark
1.2.5
.
